Much of the rest of the site is seemingly caught in the grips of the ***Magic: The Gathering** Commander* release this Friday (some of those cards are pretty cool!), but we here at Top Decks are concerned, per usual, with tournament **Magic** results.

As with many of you, I am anxiously awaiting the possible news that will come out next week RE: potential Standard bannings; these seem imminent, especially considering the not-ambiguous social communications of our beloved **Magic** boss:

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/images/magic/daily/td/td147_twitter.jpg)  

The sentiment among many players is that Caw-Blade has reached "Affinity" levels. Most of you know that I actually *like* this format and don't see a huge problem around Caw-Blade or its primary weapons, [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor) and [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic), but it is hard to argue the success that the deck has had since its inaugural Pro Tour win in the hands of Ben Stark back in Paris.

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/images/magic/daily/td/td147_jace.jpg)  

That said, I claimed a few weeks ago that the "Age of Caw-Blade" is done, and despite some continued success (e.g., PV's win in the recent Grand Prix), the deck's stranglehold seems to have loosened up. Consider the Top 8 of the most recent Star City Games Standard Open in Denver, Colorado:

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/images/magic/daily/td/td147_chart1.jpg)  

Not only did Caw-Blade fail to take 50% of the Top 8, but Brandon Montoya (Open Champion, with Vampires) and Jason Bulkowski (Red Deck) kept Caw-Blade out of the finals! Unprecedented! (Well, not really, but it's still pretty refreshing considering the last couple of months.)

### 1) So What's Going on with Vampires?

Vampires continues to succeed (with back-to-back Star City Standard Open wins) at least in part due to Caw-Blade's being optimized to beat Caw-Blade. The original Caw-Blade decks ran cards like [Sylvok Lifestaff](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sylvok+Lifestaff) to keep pace with beatdown, as well as the full four copies of [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment) to take on all comers, including little beaters. Of the three Caw-Blade variants to make the Denver Top 8, none of them played more than two copies of [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment), and only one of the three decks played [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment) main deck.

While [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) is creeping back into deck lists, the reality is that so long as Caw-Blade is optimized to beat Caw-Blade, decks like Vampires can take advantage not only of the *lack* of particular defenses, but the giant glowing neon targets that the strategy presents.

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/images/magic/daily/td/td147_vandal.jpg)  

Case in point: Montoya's three main-deck [Manic Vandal](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Manic+Vandal)s, i.e. "free value."



  

Bulkowski and Pallesen succeeded with a bit of a different deck than Red Deck mastermind Patrick Sullivan used at the Invitational a few weeks back. While the Rainmaker invented a low land count and put [Furnace Scamp](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Furnace+Scamp) on the front page, Jason and Johnathan went with an older model with 24 lands and [Koth of the Hammer](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Koth+of+the+Hammer).

<table><tbody><tr><td><div><div><img alt="Furnace Scamp" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Furnace+Scamp"></div></div></td><td><div><div><img alt="Koth of the Hammer" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Koth+of+the+Hammer"></div></div></td></tr></tbody></table>

Among new cards, [Shrine of Burning Rage](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shrine+of+Burning+Rage) has cemented itself as a red-deck staple, and [Ember Hauler](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ember+Hauler) continues to keep [Kargan Dragonlord](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kargan+Dragonlord) out of its once secure two-spot.

<table><tbody><tr><td><div><div><img alt="Shrine of Burning Rage" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Shrine+of+Burning+Rage"></div></div></td><td><div><div><img alt="Kargan Dragonlord" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Kargan+Dragonlord"></div></div></td></tr></tbody></table>

### 2) How Does the Red Deck Interact with the Caw-Blade Metagame?

For all of Caw-Blade's virtues, the strategy has a fairly basic weakness: It can largely be boiled down to a deck with about eight small guys trying to pick up one piece of equipment and quite literally "run with it."

Ignoring newcomer [Shrine of Burning Rage](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shrine+of+Burning+Rage), the standard Standard red deck plays [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning), [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt), [Searing Blaze](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Searing+Blaze), and [Staggershock](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Staggershock)... not to mention the ability on [Ember Hauler](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ember+Hauler). So, basically a 2-1 edge over Caw-Blade in terms of "efficient removal" versus "little guy" counts.

Not only can red decks get the beats on with first turn [Goblin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Guide)s getting in there, but when Caw-Blade has to devote two to four mana at sorcery speed to get a piece of Equipment into position, the deck is basically leaving itself wide open to a removal spell and a massive tempo swing.

But what about [Batterskull](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Batterskull)?

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/images/magic/daily/td/td147_batter.jpg)  

For sure [Batterskull](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Batterskull) and its lifelink can be an issue for a deck full of burn spells, but don't forget that if you kill the [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic), all of a sudden Caw-Blade has to play fairly and run out [Batterskull](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Batterskull) at the full five mana. [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning) for five mana deals with a 4/4 living weapon nicely, thank you; Red Deck can get in there with little guys and maybe a [Teetering Peaks](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Teetering+Peaks), just so.

![Batterskull](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Batterskull)



  

Alex played largely my deck from New York, albeit with a much different sideboard. The point (for this point) is that he kept the quintet of [Pilgrim's Eye](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pilgrim%27s+Eye) and [Sea Gate Oracle](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sea+Gate+Oracle). So...

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/images/magic/daily/td/td147_seaGate.jpg)  

### 3) What's the Deal With Sea Gate Oracle, Anyway?

While the biggest eyebrow-raiser in this deck is [Pilgrim's Eye](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pilgrim%27s+Eye), running a close second is brother three-drop [Sea Gate Oracle](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sea+Gate+Oracle).

<table><tbody><tr><td><div><div><img alt="Splinter Twin" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Splinter+Twin"></div></div></td><td><div><div><img alt="Sea Gate Oracle" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Sea+Gate+Oracle"></div></div></td></tr></tbody></table>

I actually borrowed the card from an extended [Pestermite](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pestermite) deck with some input from Patrick Chapin; [Sea Gate Oracle](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sea+Gate+Oracle) is a fairly intuitive addition in Standard if you consider two things: 1) sometimes you have a [Splinter Twin](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Splinter+Twin), or a spare [Splinter Twin](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Splinter+Twin), but for some reason can't go for the win (for example, you have no [Deceiver Exarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deceiver+Exarch)); in these cases, [Sea Gate Oracle](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sea+Gate+Oracle) can play "bad Jace," and better yet, a Jace that can attack (someone else's Jace), defend, and of course just dig. 2) When you have the most powerful strategy (that is, infinite damage potentially on turn four) in a format where such fast kills are absent from most other decks, maximizing the consistency and redundancy of your deck is pretty attractive.

[Sea Gate Oracle](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sea+Gate+Oracle) is particularly effective in a metagame with the rising Vampires. A 1/3 body in a world of [Pulse Tracker](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pulse+Tracker)s and [Bloodghast](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodghast)s (I mean, which deck has a good answer to [Bloodghast](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bloodghast)?) is very attractive.

Last week we looked at Michael Jacob's Caw-Blade / Exarch Twin blend, and before that have seen Blue-Red-Green (RUG) splashing [Splinter Twin](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Splinter+Twin).

Domi's is a completely different take on the colors and combo.

Instead of going with the RUG shell, Bjorn played a [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman) shell. His deck has a [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) engine, and the second- and third-best two-drops in the format as redundant turn-two plays.

![Fauna Shaman](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Fauna+Shaman)

  

Neither [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman) nor [Lotus Cobra](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lotus+Cobra) is *quite* a [Stoneforge Mystic](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stoneforge+Mystic) in the present format, but either one is a potential problem. Both of them offer explosive possibilities, from a raging stream of 4/3 Elementals to the threat of a third-turn [Frost Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Frost+Titan).

[![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/images/magic/daily/td/td147_frostPromo.jpg)](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://magic.wizards.com/en/articles/archive/duels-planeswalkers-2012-promo-cards-2011-03-30)  

At the same time, we have the possibility of a [Deceiver Exarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deceiver+Exarch)... out of nowhere. The notion of [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine)-into-[Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) setting up an infinite combo is simply delicious.

Probably my favorite thing about this deck is that it plays four [Splinter Twin](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Splinter+Twin)s (most hybrids and some Grixis decks run only three), and not only three Exarchs (thanks to [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman))... but only three copies of [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor)!

<table><tbody><tr><td><div><div><img alt="Deceiver Exarch" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Deceiver+Exarch"></div></div></td><td><div><div><img alt="Jace, the Mind Sculptor" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor"></div></div></td></tr></tbody></table>

*Ba-dum-bum!*

### 4) What Does Caw-Blade Look Like Now?

The answer is, there isn't just one answer.

However, if I were to prepare for an upcoming tournament, I would go with this style as my enemy:

#### trunks132's Caw-Blade



  

That's right! The deck played by trunks132 is a 75/75 carbon copy of the [Grand Prix](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://magic.wizards.com/en/events/coverage/da-rosa-reigns-supreme-singapore) winner piloted a week ago by Paulo Vitor Damo da Rosa.

Unique(-ish) Features:

*   [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) as a ninth creature. Previously you might have seen a [Sun Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sun+Titan) in this spot. [Sun Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sun+Titan) is nigh-unbeatable in certain situations in the mirror, but then again, sometimes it is just re-buying a lone [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk). You can't really beat a [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) after a single untap, and there is no denying its good times-ness with [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren).
*   Main-deck [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember). Good at stopping Equipment, good at stopping a [Splinter Twin](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Splinter+Twin).
*   Main-deck [Divine Offering](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Divine+Offering). Respect where respect is due.
*   Two copies of [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus). Because sometimes there is no other way to win than slapping a [Batterskull](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Batterskull) onto the tiniest poisoner.

<table><tbody><tr><td><div><div><img alt="Consecrated Sphinx" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Consecrated+Sphinx"></div></div></td><td><div><div><img alt="Dismember" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Dismember"></div></div></td></tr></tbody></table>

Paulo's deck has a miser's [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment), fast removal and an extra [Batterskull](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Batterskull) for red, and a [Sun Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sun+Titan)*anyway.* If you want to take on all comers, this is probably a good deck to do it with.

And finally...

### 5) Wasn't There a Pro Tour Last Week?

Why yes there was!

The [Pro Tour Nagoya](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://magic.wizards.com/en/events/coverage/ptnag11) Top 8 was actually Draft, but we can run a pretend-sies on the Top 8 based on what *Scars of Mirrodin* Block Constructed decks each player played.

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/images/magic/daily/td/td147_chart2.jpg)  

**[Puresteel Paladin](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Puresteel+Paladin) White Weenie or [Tempered Steel](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tempered+Steel) White Weenie?**

Half the Top 8 (including Champion David Sharfman) played some kind of White Weenie variants.

Both Pro Tour winner David Sharfman and red-hot Star City Games Invitational winner Pat Cox played the same 75.

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/images/magic/daily/td/td147_puresteel.jpg)  

The [Puresteel Paladin](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Puresteel+Paladin) style of White Weenie is a metalcraft beatdown deck that takes advantage of the speed and card advantage of [Puresteel Paladin](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Puresteel+Paladin). The deck plays lots of artifacts, including twelve main-deck Equipment cards that, in the presence of the Paladin, replace themselves and move around for free in most situations.

<table><tbody><tr><td><div><div><img alt="Puresteel Paladin" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Puresteel+Paladin"></div></div></td><td><div><div><img alt="Sword of War and Peace" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Sword+of+War+and+Peace"></div></div></td></tr></tbody></table>

Of particular note is the choice of Swords: Cox and Sharfman ran [Sword of War and Peace](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+War+and+Peace) to the max, and to the exclusion of other available Swords, probably as part of a metagame call. Remember: half the Top 8 were White Weenie variants, and another quarter were red decks.

Shards";s:22:"field\_card\_image\_front";a:0:{}}}} 

  

Pichon and LSV played [Tempered Steel](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tempered+Steel) decks that were actually quite a bit different on the details. While both players ran [Glint Hawk Idol](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Glint+Hawk+Idol), only one ran [Glint Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Glint+Hawk); Scott-Vargas ran [Origin Spellbomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Origin+Spellbomb) and more lands, as well as [Blade Splicer](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blade+Splicer) over more [Vault Skirge](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vault+Skirge)s.

<table><tbody><tr><td><div><div><img alt="Glint Hawk" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Glint+Hawk"></div></div></td><td><div><div><img alt="Blade Splicer" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Blade+Splicer"></div></div></td></tr></tbody></table>

Despite some differences on the details, the theme of the [Tempered Steel](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tempered+Steel) strategy is pretty consistent: Cast lots of little artifact guys, pump them large with [Tempered Steel](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tempered+Steel), smash.

[Dispatch](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dispatch) is a superb tool here (and in [Puresteel Paladin](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Puresteel+Paladin) White Weenie, for that matter) as a kind of no-downside [Swords to Plowshares](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swords+to+Plowshares). You basically always have metalcraft; you just do it. For one.



  

Toshiyuki Kadooka played an aggressive Kuldotha Red deck.

In addition to an eight-pack of battle cry beaters that work well with [Kuldotha Rebirth](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kuldotha+Rebirth), Kadooka ran a nice sub-theme of artifacts and profitable ways to sacrifice them.

<table><tbody><tr><td><div><div><img alt="Kuldotha Rebirth" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Kuldotha+Rebirth"></div></div></td><td><div><div><img alt="Ichor Wellspring" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Ichor+Wellspring"></div></div></td></tr></tbody></table>

[Kuldotha Rebirth](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kuldotha+Rebirth) + [Ichor Wellspring](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ichor+Wellspring) is obvious, but you can also draw a card from [Artillerize](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Artillerize), or run [Panic Spellbomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Panic+Spellbomb) the same way. [Artillerize](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Artillerize) + [Perilous Myr](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Perilous+Myr) is probably one of the worst ways you can ever lose.

<table><tbody><tr><td><div><div><img alt="Artillerize" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Artillerize"></div></div></td><td><div><div><img alt="Perilous Myr" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Perilous+Myr"></div></div></td></tr></tbody></table>

"What are you at again? Seven?"

Awkward.

Refugee";s:22:"field\_card\_image\_front";a:0:{}}}} 

  

Same color, same planeswalker... couldn't be more different.

Fabian played a midrange (yet still explosive) deck with lots of fours and a big five. [Galvanic Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Galvanic+Blast) is often only 2 damage in this deck, but still fast. Thiele makes up the burn count with Koth-friendly (and [Sphere of the Suns](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sphere+of+the+Suns)–friendly) [Volt Charge](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volt+Charge) and takes advantage of tons of mana with [Red Sun's Zenith](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Red+Sun%27s+Zenith).

Times four!



  

Of note:

*   Twenty-three lands plus [Mycosynth Wellspring](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mycosynth+Wellspring).
*   [Mycosynth Wellspring](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mycosynth+Wellspring) plus [Beast Within](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Beast+Within) (probably during combat!).
*   Anything plus [Tezzeret, Agent of Bolas](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tezzeret%2C+Agent+of+Bolas).
*   [Thrun, the Last Troll](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thrun%2C+the+Last+Troll) into [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) into [Karn Liberated](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Karn+Liberated)?!?

<table><tbody><tr><td><div><div><img alt="Thrun, the Last Troll" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Thrun%2C+the+Last+Troll"></div></div></td><td><div><div><img alt="Karn Liberated" src="http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&amp;name=Karn+Liberated"></div></div></td></tr></tbody></table>

It's not hard to see how the man was at one point the consensus best deck designer in the world.

Respect.



  

Grand Prix winner and general awesome dude standout Gaudenis Vidurgiris went with a straightforward and effective linear strategy.

There are lots of awesome things going on with this deck in the metagame, starting with [Virulent Wound](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Virulent+Wound) versus [Memnite](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Memnite) (or whatever). But the best has to be the positioning of [Phyrexian Crusader](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Phyrexian+Crusader) in a room full of white and red decks.

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/images/magic/daily/td/td147_pCrusader.jpg)  

[Phyrexian Crusader](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Phyrexian+Crusader), untap, [Piston Sledge](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Piston+Sledge)? No one can block it. No one can remove it. [Mutagenic Growth](https://web.archive.org/web/20110616000000/https://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mutagenic+Growth) you? Wow. Just wow.

![](https://media.magic.wizards.com/image_legacy_migration/images/magic/daily/td/td147_gameState.jpg)  

Block Constructed is often a window into future Standard formats. Especially if something happens next Monday, these decks should prove building blocks for the near future.

Congratulations to all the Nagoya standouts!
